This test rig consists of a primary working and a secondary cooling-filtration circuit which are connected via the oil tank [1], [2]. Moreover, 17 additional measurement profiles were added, expanding the dataset from 138 hours to 185 hours of records.\n\n## Context\n\nThe data set comprises several sensor data collected from a permanent magnet synchronous motor (PMSM) deployed on a test bench. The PMSM represents a german OEM's prototype model. Test bench measurements were collected by the LEA department at Paderborn University.\n\n## Content\n\nAll recordings are sampled at 2 Hz. The data set consists of multiple measurement sessions, which can be distinguished from each other by column \"profile_id\". A measurement session can be between one and six hours long.\n\nThe motor is excited by hand-designed driving cycles denoting a reference motor speed and a reference torque.\nCurrents in d/q-coordinates (columns \"i_d\" and i_q\") and voltages in d/q-coordinates (columns \"u_d\" and \"u_q\") are a result of a standard control strategy trying to follow the reference speed and torque.\nColumns \"motor_speed\" and \"torque\" are the resulting quantities achieved by that strategy, derived from set currents and voltages.\n\n\n## Acknowledgements\n\nSeveral publications leveraged the setup of the PMSM in the Paderborn University Lab:\n\n### Temperature Estimation on the same motor but different data\n\n  * Determination of rotor temperature for an interior permanent magnet synchronous machine using a precise flux observer\n  * Investigation of Long Short-Term Memory Networks to Temperature Prediction for Permanent Magnet Synchronous Motors\n  * Real-Time Capable Methods to Determine the Magnet Temperature of Permanent Magnet Synchronous Motors — A Review\n  * Improved Fusion of Permanent Magnet Temperature Estimation Techniques for Synchronous Motors Using a Kalman Filter\n  * Fusion of a lumped-parameter thermal network and speed-dependent flux observer for PM temperature estimation in synchronous machines\n  * Fusion of direct and indirect temperature estimation techniques for permanent magnet synchronous motors\n  * Sensitivity analysis of a permanent magnet temperature observer for PM synchronous machines using the Monte Carlo method\n  * Observing the Permanent-Magnet Temperature of Synchronous Motors Based on Electrical Fundamental Wave Model Quantities\n  * Global Identification of a Low-Order Lumped-Parameter Thermal Network for Permanent Magnet Synchronous Motors\n  * Glocal identification methods for low-order lumped parameter thermal networks used in permanent magnet synchronous motors\n\n### Temperature Estimation based on this dataset\n\nPlease cite the following publication if you intend to use this dataset for your own publications:\n\n  * Estimating Electric Motor Temperatures with Deep Residual Machine Learning.\nCode available on GitHub upb-lea/deep-pmsm\n\n## Inspiration\n\nThe most interesting target features are rotor temperature (\"pm\"), stator temperatures (\"stator_*\") and torque.\nEspecially rotor temperature and torque are not reliably and economically measurable in a commercial vehicle.\n\nBeing able to have strong estimators for the rotor temperature helps the automotive industry to manufacture motors with less material and enables control strategies to utilize the motor to its maximum capability.\nA precise torque estimate leads to more accurate and adequate control of the motor, reducing power losses and eventually heat build-up.\n\n## Other electric motor related data sets\n\n  * Voltage converter data\n  * Currents and motor speed per Kindly follow the protocol mentioned in the paper (using the first three years' data for training/ cross-validation and the last two for testing) for reproducibility and comparability of works. # Production Plant Data for Condition Monitoring ... ## Production Plant Data for Condition Monitoring ... ## About Dataset\n\n# Production Plant Data for Condition Monitoring\n\nPrediction of the condition of an important component\n\nImage\n\n## Production Plant Data for Condition Monitoring\n\nData CardCode (2)Discussion (7)Suggestions (0)\n\n## About Dataset\n\n### Context\n\nThis dataset was used in previous research projects, for example in IMPROVE.\n\n### Content\n\nThe use case focuses on the prediction of the condition of an important component within production lines.\nThe condition of this component is important for the function of the plant and the resulting product quality.\nData for 8 run-to-failure experiments were provided and 8 features related to the component were selected.\nTraining and prediction data were selected using the leave-one-out method:\ndata from the component under test were selected as the target for the prediction.\nA set amount of data of all other components were selected and combined to serve as training data for the 'new' condition.\nA SOM was trained on a training data to represent the 'new' condition.\nThe degradation of the component under test was calculated and visualized.\nThis procedure was repeated for all 8 data sets to get a prediction of the degradation for all components.\nThe prediction worked for all cases which were labeled with a certain type of wear by experts.\nFurthermore, one of the components did not show signs of wear according to experts which was also confirmed by the model.\n\n### Acknowledgements\n\nThis dataset is publicly available for anyone to use under the following terms.\n\nvon Birgelen, Alexander; El syllabus también requiere otras nociones durante el semestre, pero no se marcaron como ya cursadas. Python se aprende practicando en notebooks; los quizzes conceptuales de esta página no exigen sintaxis Python.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Precisiones matemáticas y de código\\r\\n\\r\\nEstas son aclaraciones añadidas por el portal, no modificaciones de los archivos del profesor:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **M1 §1.4:** una matriz de vectores de características aparece rotulada `y`; se presenta correctamente como `X` y las etiquetas como `y`.\\r\\n2. **M2 §2.2:** la norma de Minkowski general necesita subíndice `p`; para ser una métrica se requiere `p≥1`. Para `0<p<1` la expresión no cumple desigualdad triangular.\\r\\n3. **M2 §2.8:** calcular todas las distancias cuesta O(nd), y ordenar todas añade O(n log n). La búsqueda eficiente por índices depende de geometría y dimensión; no se promete O(d log n) universal.\\r\\n4. **Assignment 1, celda 85:** `c >= k//2` no implementa mayoría estricta. Para k=5, dos votos positivos ya ganan; para k=1, incluso cero votos positivos ganan. El portal enseña mayoría correcta y una política explícita de empates. La condición también está en la copia personal y se conserva intacta.\\r\\n5. **Assignment 1, texto sobre reshape:** reshape conserva el número de elementos. La repetición/truncamiento corresponde a resize; no son intercambiables.\\r\\n6. **M3 §3.2.1:** MSE de regresión lineal es convexa, pero unicidad de los coeficientes requiere rango completo de la matriz de diseño. El mínimo no tiene por qué ser cero.\\r\\n7. **M3 §3.3.1:** para f(t)=t², t siguiente=(1−2η)t: converge con 0<η<1, oscila con η=1 desde un punto no nulo y diverge con η>1. No extrapolar ese límite a pérdidas diferentes.\\r\\n8. **M3 §3.3.2:** f(a,b)=a²+3ab+b²+3 sirve para derivar parcialmente, pero no debe ilustrar una función con mínimo: su Hessiano tiene eigenvalores 5 y −1. Las demos usan MSE convexa.\\r\\n9. **M3 §3.5:** el módulo absorbe 2/n en la tasa ρ. El portal mantiene el factor explícito y explica la diferencia para que tasas numéricas no se comparen directamente.\\r\\n10. **Tutoría 2 A:** los pesos e intercepto son parámetros; η, epochs y tamaño de lote son hiperparámetros. Para condiciones estacionarias se iguala a cero la primera derivada; PCA no equivale al ajuste OLS por ecuaciones normales.\\r\\n11. **M3 §3.8:** R² puede ser negativo. El denominador de R² estándar usa la media del conjunto evaluado; una baseline desplegable se entrena con la media de train. Target constante necesita tratamiento especial.\\r\\n12. **M3 §3.9:** se explica el bucle de propuestas de RANSAC además del pseudocódigo abreviado del módulo.\\r\\n13. **M3 §3.10:** una banda basada en desviación incluye raíz; no es literalmente una característica polinómica cuadrática. No se convierte ese ejemplo en consejo financiero.\\r\\n14. **Tutoría 2 B:** las fronteras entre rombo y círculo corresponden a 1<p<2. El experimento de la cáscara usa valores recalculados y declara la hipótesis uniforme; no afirma que toda distancia sea inútil en dimensión alta.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Lecturas complementarias\\r\\n\\r\\n\"}"

This PMSM represents a traction drive from a german manufacturer. Test bench measurements were collected by the LEA department at Paderborn University.\n\n### Content\n\nEach row of the 40 mil. samples shows currents (in d/q-coordinates) flowing through the motor's stator windings before and after a certain switching state within the pre-aligned inverter is applied. \"epsilon_k\" stands for the rotor angle at this moment. The inverter is a power electronic component deciding how the battery voltage (so called dc-link voltage) is applied on the three phase circuits of the PMSM.\n\nRows follow no particular order.\n\n### Inspiration\n\nThe most important aspect of an electric vehicle from a marketing and engineering perspective is its efficiency and, thus, achievable range.\nFor this, it is essential to avoid over-dimensioning of the drive train, i.e. applying more and heavier metal packs to increase its thermal capabilities.\nIf the motor is controlled inefficiently through the inverter, there'll be superfluous power losses, i.e. heat build-up, which eventually leads to electric power derating during operation and, crucially, early depletion of the battery.\n\nEfficient control can be achieved only if the dynamic behavior of the drive train is accurately known by the control algorithm.\nThe task of the controller is to achieve a desired torque at the vehicle's wheels by controlling the currents of the electric motor via the applied voltages.\nThe most interesting task here is hence to be able to accurately predict the currents for a given switching command (also called element vector).\n\n### Detailed description\n\nFor more details see the two parts of the paper \"Data Set Description: Identifying the Physics Behind an Electric Motor - # 🚅MetroPT-3 Train Dataset ... Data Card Code (6)Discussion (3)Suggestions (0)\n\n# 🚅MetroPT-3 Train Dataset\n\nReadings from pressure, temperature, motor current, and air intake valves\n\nImage\n\n## 🚅MetroPT-3 Train Dataset\n\nData CardCode (6)Discussion (3)Suggestions (0)\n\n## About Dataset\n\n# Description\n\n> This MetroPT-3 Dataset contains readings from pressure, temperature, motor current, and air intake valves were collected from a compressor's Air Production Unit (APU) from a metro train. This dataset reveals real predictive maintenance challenges encountered in the industry .It consists of multivariate time series data obtained from several analogue and digital sensors installed on the compressor of a train.\n\n# Key Features\n\n> The dataset consists of 15169480 data points collected at 1Hz from February to August 2020 and is described by 15\n> features from 7 analogue (1-7) and 8 digital (8-15) sensors:\n> \n>   1. TP2 (bar) – the measure of the pressure on the compressor.\n>   2. TP3 (bar) – the measure of the pressure generated at the pneumatic panel.\n>   3. H1 (bar) – the measure of the pressure generated due to pressure drop when the discharge of the cyclonic\n> separator filter occurs.\n>   4. DV pressure (bar) – the measure of the pressure drop generated when the towers discharge air dryers; a zero\n> reading indicates that the compressor is operating under load.\n>   5. Reservoirs (bar) – the measure of the downstream pressure of the reservoirs, which should be close to the\n> pneumatic panel pressure (TP3).\n>   6. Motor Current (A) – the measure of the current of one phase of the three-phase motor; it presents values close to\n> 0A - when it turns off, 4A - when working offloaded, 7A - when working under load, and 9A - when it starts\n> working.\n>   7. Oil Temperature (ºC) – the measure of the oil temperature on the compressor.\n>   8. COMP - the electrical signal of the air intake valve on the compressor; it is active when there is no air intake,\n> indicating that the compressor is either turned off or operating in an offloaded state.\n>   9. DV electric – the electrical signal that controls the compressor outlet valve; it is active when the compressor is\n> functioning under load and inactive when the compressor is either off or operating in an offloaded state.\n>   10. TOWERS – the electrical signal that defines the tower responsible for drying the air and the tower responsible\n> for draining the humidity removed from the air; when not active, it indicates that tower one is functioning; when\n> active, it indicates that tower two is in operation.\n>   11. MPG – the electrical signal responsible for starting the compressor under load by activating the intake valve\n> when the pressure in the air production unit (APU) falls below 8.2 bar; it activates the COMP sensor, which assumes\n> the same behaviour as the MPG sensor.\n>   12. LPS – the electrical signal that detects and activates when the pressure drops below 7 bars.\n>   13. Pressure Switch - the electrical signal that detects the discharge in the air-drying towers.\n>   14. Oil Level – the electrical signal that detects the oil level on the compressor; it is active when the oil is below the\n> expected values.\n>   15. Caudal Impulse – the electrical signal that counts the pulse outputs generated by the absolute amount of air\n> flowing from the APU to the reservoirs.\n\n# Use cases\n\n> It can be used for failure predictions, anomaly explanations, and other tasks\n> This dataset can be used for predictive maintenance, anomaly detection, and remaining\n> useful life (RUL) prediction models for compressors using deep learning and machine learning methods\n\n# Feel free to download the data and use it in your work. I will wait for interesting notebooks from your side. Thank you\n\n## License\n\nAttribution 4.0 International (CC BY 4.0)\n\n## Tags\n\nBusinessClassificationComputer ScienceEducationTransportationTime Series Analysis\n\n## MetroPT3(AirCompressor).csv(218.3 MB)\n\nget_app\n\nfullscreen\n\nchevron_right\n\nLoading...\n\n## Data Explorer\n\n[Button: Version 1] (218.3 MB)\n\n  * calendar_view_week\n\nMetroPT3(AirCompressor).csv\n\n## Summary\n\narrow_right\n\nfolder\n\n1 file\n\narrow_right\n\ncalendar_view_week\n\n17 columns\n\nlightbulb\n\n## Activity Overview\n\nvisibility\n\n### Views\n\n21.8K\n\ntrending_up 348 in the last 30 days\n\ndownload\n\n### Downloads\n\n2441\n\ntrending_up 47 in the last 30 days--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\nElectric Motor Temperature (https://www.kaggle.com/wkirgsn/electric-motor-temperature/metadata)\nciteturn8search6 [wordlim: 200] Published: 5.4 years ago; Crawled: last week; Regression Model between Electric Motor Parameters more_vert Updated 7 years ago Electric Motor Temperature\n\n# Electric Motor Temperature\n\n185 hrs recordings from a permanent magnet synchronous motor (PMSM)\n\nImage\n\n## Electric Motor Temperature\n\nData CardCode (96)Discussion (31)Suggestions (0)\n\n## About Dataset\n\nImage: titlepage header\n\n## UPDATE 26.04.2021\n\nAll data is deanonymized now. Moreover, 17 additional measurement profiles were added, expanding the dataset from 138 hours to 185 hours of records.\n\n## Context\n\nThe data set comprises several sensor data collected from a permanent magnet synchronous motor (PMSM) deployed on a test bench. The PMSM represents a german OEM's prototype model. Test bench measurements were collected by the LEA department at Paderborn University.\n\n## Content\n\nAll recordings are sampled at 2 Hz. The data set consists of multiple measurement sessions, which can be distinguished from each other by column \"profile_id\". A measurement session can be between one and six hours long.\n\nThe motor is excited by hand-designed driving cycles denoting a reference motor speed and a reference torque.\nCurrents in d/q-coordinates (columns \"i_d\" and i_q\") and voltages in d/q-coordinates (columns \"u_d\" and \"u_q\") are a result of a standard control strategy trying to follow the reference speed and torque.\nColumns \"motor_speed\" and \"torque\" are the resulting quantities achieved by that strategy, derived from set currents and voltages.\n\n\n## Acknowledgements\n\nSeveral publications leveraged the setup of the PMSM in the Paderborn University Lab:\n\n### Temperature Estimation on the same motor but different data\n\n  * Determination of rotor temperature for an interior permanent magnet synchronous machine using a precise flux observer\n  * Investigation of Long Short-Term Memory Networks to Temperature Prediction for Permanent Magnet Synchronous Motors\n  * Real-Time Capable Methods to Determine the Magnet Temperature of Permanent Magnet Synchronous Motors — A Review\n  * Improved Fusion of Permanent Magnet Temperature Estimation Techniques for Synchronous Motors Using a Kalman Filter\n  * Fusion of a lumped-parameter thermal network and speed-dependent flux observer for PM temperature estimation in synchronous machines\n  * Fusion of direct and indirect temperature estimation techniques for permanent magnet synchronous motors\n  * Sensitivity analysis of a permanent magnet temperature observer for PM synchronous machines using the Monte Carlo method\n  * Observing the Permanent-Magnet Temperature of Synchronous Motors Based on Electrical Fundamental Wave Model Quantities\n  * Global Identification of a Low-Order Lumped-Parameter Thermal Network for Permanent Magnet Synchronous Motors\n  * Glocal identification methods for low-order lumped parameter thermal networks used in permanent magnet synchronous motors\n\n### Temperature Estimation based on this dataset\n\nPlease cite the following publication if you intend to use this dataset for your own publications:\n\n  * Estimating Electric Motor Temperatures with Deep Residual Machine Learning.\nCode available on GitHub upb-lea/deep-pmsm\n\n## Inspiration\n\nThe most interesting target features are rotor temperature (\"pm\"), stator temperatures (\"stator_*\") and torque.\nEspecially rotor temperature and torque are not reliably and economically measurable in a commercial vehicle.\n\nBeing able to have strong estimators for the rotor temperature helps the automotive industry to manufacture motors with less material and enables control strategies to utilize the motor to its maximum capability.\nA precise torque estimate leads to more accurate and adequate control of the motor, reducing power losses and eventually heat build-up.\n\n## Other electric motor related data sets\n\n  * Voltage converter data\n  * Currents and motor speed per switching state\n  * Torque characteristics\n\nexpand_more View more\n\n## Usability\n\ninfo\n\n10.00\n\n## License\n\nCC BY-SA 4.0\n\n## Expected update frequency\n\nNever\n\n## Tags\n\nAutomobiles and VehiclesElectronicsTime Series AnalysisNeural NetworksEngineering\n\n## measures_v2.csv(300.06 MB)\n\nAbout this file\n\nA comprehensive csv file containing all measurement sessions and features.\nEach row represents one snapshot of sensor data at a certain time step.\nSample rate is 2 Hz (One row per 0.5 seconds).\nDistinctive sessions are identified with \"profile_id\".\n\nVoltage q-component measurement in dq-coordinates (in V)\n\ngrid_3x3 coolant sort\n\nCoolant temperature (in °C)\n\ngrid_3x3 stator_winding sort\n\nStator winding temperature (in °C) measured with thermocouples\n\ngrid_3x3 u_d sort\n\nVoltage d-component measurement in dq-coordinates\n\ngrid_3x3 stator_tooth sort\n\nStator tooth temperature (in °C) measured with thermocouples\n\ngrid_3x3 motor_speed sort\n\nMotor speed (in rpm)\n\ngrid_3x3 i_d sort\n\nCurrent d-component measurement in dq-coordinates\n\ngrid_3x3 i_q sort\n\nCurrent q-component measurement in dq-coordinates\n\ngrid_3x3 pm sort\n\nPermanent magnet temperature (in °C) measured with thermocouples and transmitted wirelessly via a thermography unit.\n\ngrid_3x3 stator_yoke sort\n\nStator yoke temperature (in °C) measured with thermocouples\n\n## Data Explorer\n\n[Button: Version 3] (300.06 MB)\n\n  * calendar_view_week\n\nmeasures_v2.csv\n\n## Summary\n\narrow_right\n\nfolder\n\n1
